NUM	<p>6ec4_bf92</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #626200;">Gene J</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #626200;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #626200;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #365ca4;">Gene M</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #365ca4;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #365ca4;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #993260;">Gene Y</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #993260;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #993260;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>0d44_d38f</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #92441c;">Gene C</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #92441c;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene X</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #574eb0;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>d8c5_8adc</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #436b18;">Gene A</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #436b18;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>8fa6_116e</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene J</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #a32c31;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene W</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006b6f;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene Y</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #574eb0;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>3af9_c36d</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #864f00;">Gene H</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #864f00;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #864f00;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006980;">Gene N</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006980;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006980;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene R</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #8a3984;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>6a02_d38f</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene N</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #a32c31;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene X</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #006b6f;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene Y</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #574eb0;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>7bba_17ef</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #626200;">Gene K</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #626200;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #626200;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #365ca4;">Gene R</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #365ca4;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #365ca4;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #993260;">Gene X</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #993260;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #993260;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; NUM	<p>8ca8_98d7</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #626200;">Gene E</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #626200;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #993260;">Gene T</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #993260;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #993260;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>0109_7d73</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #864f00;">Gene E</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #864f00;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #864f00;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006e5b;">Gene P</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006e5b;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e5b;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene T</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #8a3984;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>74de_b913</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #765900;">Gene A</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #765900;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #765900;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006e5b;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006e5b;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e5b;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene W</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #574eb0;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>4c39_d38f</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #436b18;">Gene C</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #436b18;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #436b18;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006980;">Gene M</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006980;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006980;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #993260;">Gene W</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #993260;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #993260;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>6395_1506</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #a32c31;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #626200;">Gene E</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #626200;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #626200;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006497;">Gene J</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006497;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006497;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>ee15_9e56</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #864f00;">Gene C</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #864f00;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #864f00;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene E</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #006b6f;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #783baa;">Gene H</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #783baa;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #783baa;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>e893_9e56</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #436b18;">Gene B</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #436b18;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #436b18;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006497;">Gene C</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #006497;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006497;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene T</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #8a3984;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>75a8_a9f1</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #92441c;">Gene D</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #92441c;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #92441c;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006980;">Gene P</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #006980;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006980;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #783baa;">Gene T</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #783baa;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #783baa;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>6b40_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #864f00;">Gene C</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #864f00;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006b6f;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #574eb0;">Gene Y</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #574eb0;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>bfa0_070d</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #864f00;">Gene B</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #864f00;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #864f00;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006e5b;">Gene F</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006e5b;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e5b;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene J</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #8a3984;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a3984;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>02cb_98d7</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene A</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #a32c31;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a32c31;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #436b18;">Gene H</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #436b18;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #436b18;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006497;">Gene T</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006497;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006497;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse;
